## 2026-09-25 (remediation) — handoff review defects fixed + oracle truth pass

The reviewer rejected the U0–U12 completion claim on five counts; all
were confirmed and addressed. Status language below is scoped to what
the assertions now actually prove.

### Editing defects fixed in production (`src/lib/`)

- **Nested-boundary delete left doomed blocks alive**
  (`edytor.utils.ts`): the ancestor-rescue branch removed only
  `deletedEndAncestor` and returned — the head classified for deletion
  (plus other doomed interior blocks) survived. Now removes every doomed
  block not inside the rescued subtree. `del.range.nested-tail` is no
  longer asymmetric.
- **Dead-endpoint recovery could strand the caret on a dead subtree**
  (`edytor.utils.ts` seam unification + `selection.svelte.ts`): repair
  now climbs to the topmost dead child of the nearest live ancestor and
  resolves the vacated slot (forward sibling's first text @0, previous
  sibling's last text @end, else the first real child's first text) —
  replacing the phantom-root fallback whose caret looked live but
  dropped the next keystroke. Follow-up typing at the recovered caret is
  asserted, not just liveness. `sel.seam.nested-subtree` resolved.

### Harness corrections (tests/ + src/tests/)

- **Deterministic command simulator** (`command-peer-set.ts` +
  `deterministic-command-scenario.ts`): mounted editor peers on a real
  `PeerSet` — deterministic clientIDs/rand/IDs, explicit edge delivery,
  partition + heal, virtual-time undo capture windows, persistence +
  snapshot reconstruction, `command` intent records in the trace, and a
  fresh-process fingerprint proof over the mounted-editor program.
- **Trace evidence** (applied by user): `h` carries ordered FNV-1a
  update-payload hashes and `d` records delivery/reload/sync decisions;
  same-length payloads and withheld-vs-applied choices now diverge the
  trace. Fresh-process probe writes digests via `process.stdout.write`
  - `--disableConsoleIntercept`.
- **Canonical-tree oracle** (`test.utils.ts`): `assertCanonicalTree`
  compares `{type, id, data, content, children}` recursively — replaces
  flattened `texts()` in golden programs; identity checks use
  `blockIdMap`. Canaries now feed real corruption through the shared
  oracle. Reversed selections use `Selection.setBaseAndExtent`.
- **Word-delete oracle truth** (`deleteOracle.ts`): discovered and
  documented — `deleteWord*` is not a deleteTargetRangeInput, so the
  browser's delivered `targetRange` is synced INTO the model selection
  and `deleteContent*` consumes it: the platform-declared unit IS the
  contract (`del.unit.adopted-range`). The independent bound is
  plausibility — caret-anchored over a collapsed caret, at most one
  contract word-run — violations are `delete-range-implausible`.
  Collapsed/absent ranges keep the model-boundary contract
  (`del.unit.model-boundary`). Non-collapsed selections accept
  `deleteContent*` intent (the selection is the unit).
- **Passive-peer exactness** (`collab-runner.ts` + page dump): the
  endpoint's `TextAnchor` is captured pre-step, self-consistency-checked
  (`*AnchorOk`), and re-resolved on the converged doc — production's
  recovery path verbatim — plus the dead-block repair seam and the
  untouched-endpoint no-movement case as the acceptable set; zero remote
  frames asserts no movement, multi-frame steps defer (chained recovery
  isn't externally replayable). `firstMismatchPath` now reports the true
  divergent sibling instead of recursing into equal prefixes.

### Validation (remediation round)

- `pnpm check`/`pnpm lint` clean; `pnpm test:dom` 314/314;
  `transforms.test.ts` 109/109; CRDT harness 12/12 incl. fresh-process.
- DST solo seeds 1–8 × 40 steps: 8/8. Collab seeds 1–6 + held-window:
  7/7 — with the stricter passive-exactness and adopted-range oracle
  live. Oracle specs 27/27 (incl. adopted/oversized/unanchored pins).

### Remaining qualified bounds

- Passive exactness is bounded to single-remote-frame steps; multi-frame
  chains assert liveness+bounds only.
- `conc.delete-wins-block` remains the documented tombstone-wins policy.
- Word/line unit _content_ is platform-owned; the oracle bounds
  plausibility rather than re-deriving ICU segmentation.

## 2026-09-25 (remediation II) — stale-selection writers + oracle counterexample round

A second adversarial review reproduced one live production defect
(stale caret after remote edits inside the mutation-repair window) and
confirmed oracle/harness blind spots. This round pinned each
counterexample first, then fixed production, then re-verified.

### Production fixes (`src/lib/`)

- **`selection.svelte.ts` — `setAtTextOffset` staleness guard**: the
  collapsed write path now aborts when the live state differs from BOTH
  the write target AND the call-time fields — the `setAtRange` twin's
  guard. A `restoreRelativePosition` write resolved against the OLD
  anchor position (`@7`) was committing after the DOM-derive had already
  re-resolved the caret to `@12` — visible in the minimized collab
  replay as a stale in-flight write.
- **`domTextMutationObserver.ts` — `restoreSelectionAfterRepair`**
  restores through the endpoint anchor that was live when the repair
  flush captured (`state.relativePosition` at capture time) instead of
  the absolute `(startText, yStart)` pair. Minting a fresh anchor at
  capture time resolves to the pre-resolution stale offset — the
  remote commit lands BEFORE the flush runs. The pre-existing endpoint
  anchor resolves to the user's real position through remote commits
  (insert-before-caret shifts it) and to the pre-repair caret through
  commit-free renderer churn.

### Oracle / harness corrections (`tests/`)

- **`collab-runner.ts` passive-selection oracle** delegates endpoint
  acceptability to `selectionOracle.ts` (pure module — pinned by unit
  tests): acceptable spots = resolved-endpoint-anchor (consistency
  gated) ∪ dead-block seam (owner-id correct) ∪ surviving-untouched
  absolute position. Structural invariants reject vanished selections
  and collapsed→range transitions; zero remote frames asserts NO
  movement; surviving-block preservation now requires the endpoint
  textId's content unchanged (`textUntouched`), closing the
  "block survived → old offset OK" hole.
- **Page dump (`src/routes/test/dom/+page.svelte`)** records each
  text part's OWNING block id (`firstOwner`/`lastOwner`) and a
  `textContents` map — the seam expectation compares owner ids, fixing
  the false-negative where `block.firstText` descends into a child.
- **`deleteOracle.ts` word-delete bound**: `wordRunsInRange` resets
  `inWord` at block boundaries (two paragraphs' words are two runs) and
  `truncatesWordRun` rejects spans splitting a contract run
  (`alpha bravo|`→`alpha brav` rejected). Same-part ASCII spans get
  INDEPENDENT expected-unit matching (`legalWordSpans` — the contract
  word unit or the boundary-run alone); non-ASCII/cross-part spans get
  the plausibility bound (anchored, ≤1 run, no truncation). Phantom
  spans (WebKit delivers a ZWSP placeholder as `[0,1)` over an empty
  text part) skip strict matching via an in-model-bounds gate and fall
  to plausibility.
- **Canonical convergence**: `canonicalBlock`/`assertCanonicalTree`
  gained `withIds` (block + text part ids); all nine collaboration
  command programs and the deterministic scenario compare recursive
  canonical trees — peer convergence is id-pinned, replacing every
  flattened `textContents`/`texts` assertion.
- **Generated schedules + quiescence** (`command-schedules.test.ts`):
  generated multi-step command schedules run on `PeerSet` with
  byte-identical trace + id-pinned signature comparison, in-process
  repeats and fresh-process fingerprints. `command-peer-set.ts`
  `quiesce()` accounts for REAL timers — drains settlement-horizon
  callbacks (≤250ms) while recording out-of-horizon suppression timers
  as stable evidence (no nondeterministic counts in the trace).
  Fake timers were rejected: `flushDomUpdates` awaits real
  `setTimeout(0)`.
- **`edytor.svelte.ts` focusin restore** re-targets the live state at
  fire time instead of replaying the focus-time cache (same stale-write
  class as the two confirmed vandals).

### Validation (remediation II)

- `pnpm check` 0 errors/warnings; `pnpm lint` clean;
  `pnpm test` 1939 tests (123 files).
- `pnpm test:dst` **52/52**: 33 oracle specs (incl. new counterexample
  pins — truncated run, cross-block run, vanished selection,
  collapsed→range, phantom ZWSP span), 6 collab seeds + held-window,
  8 solo seeds across chromium+webkit.
- The previously failing minimized collab artifact
  (`seed-1-empty-and-plain`, caret `@7` stranded vs `@12`) replays
  green; the three webkit `delete-range-implausible` artifacts
  (phantom ZWSP spans) replay green.

### Remaining qualified bounds (unchanged + one new)

- Passive exactness remains bounded to single-remote-frame steps.
- Word/line unit content stays platform-owned; phantom DOM-space spans
  (offsets beyond part length on empty texts) are bounded by
  plausibility, not strict unit matching.
- `conc.delete-wins-block` remains the documented tombstone-wins
  policy.

## 2026-09-25 (remediation III) — seg-boundary anchor escape + recovery hardening round

Root cause found via page-side selection-write spy on the minimized
collab artifact (seed 6 `void-neighbours`): a caret minted at a fresh
split block's text offset 0 left-bound its anchor to the previous
block's last atom (`{b: dst-6-b1, i: e-atom, a: -1}`) — the anchor
resolved to `b1@4` while the state legitimately sat at `b_g82@0`. The
state and its own anchor disagreed from birth; the passive oracle's
anchor-exclusive contract correctly flagged it. A naive affinity flip
(offset-0 → 'right') fixed the DST but broke composition restores —
`_compositionPin.caretAnchor` relies on left-association (anchor stays
before composition inserts; the failing `nにello` ordering).

Fix landed in `facade.anchorAt` (`edytor-doc.ts`): left-affinity at a
seg start now binds the previous atom **of the block's own display
stream** (the previous `flatten` seg's last atom) rather than the
backing-text neighbour — which can be foreign-owned. At the block's
own stream start, `seg.i0 === 0` keeps the live-start sentinel
(composition semantics preserved); `seg.i0 > 0` right-binds the seg's
first atom — the only own-facet encoding (assoc-coupled insert
semantics at that exact seam accepted: remote inserts land before the
caret, which is the correct tracking there anyway). Slice-record
`anchorAt` (engine-internal) untouched; the facade's `anchorAt` has
exactly two callers, both in `selection.svelte.ts`.

Companion fix in `restoreRelativePosition`: an endpoint whose anchor
migrates into a _touched_ text while its own wrapper stays live (merge
claims the bound atoms into another text) now follows the resolution —
previously `touchesStart/touchesEnd` alone gated the restore, stranding
carets on emptied-but-live wrappers that `restoreDeadSelectionEndpoints`
also skips (liveness is intact).

Also this round (evidence from the earlier write-spy trace, kept from
the pre-revert work): editable seam recovery via `_dropNext`/`_dropPrev`

- `firstEditableText`/`lastEditableText`, gesture-serial stale-write
  guards on `setAtTextOffset`/`setAtRange`/`setAtBlockRange`,
  `expectInternalFocus` markers on programmatic focus, anchor-identity
  oracle (`textIdAlive`/resolved-anchor exclusivity, no seam union,
  resolver exceptions fail), per-command independent expectations +
  per-step quiesce in generated schedules, delay-class timer accounting
  (callbacks rescheduling short callbacks can no longer escape a fixed
  deadline), narrowed word-oracle phantom gate + block/atom-boundary run
  resets, corrected `alXXXXXpha-bavo` expectation.

### Validation (remediation III)

- `pnpm check` 0 errors/warnings; `pnpm lint` clean.
- `pnpm test:dom` **330/330** (21 files) — including the two composition
  fixtures that caught the naive affinity flip.
- `pnpm test:dst` **54/54** — the failing seed-6 `void-neighbours`
  collab schedule (31 steps) and its minimized replay both green.
- `command-schedules` 8/8 incl. the all-commands-disabled no-op proof.

### Remaining qualified bounds

- Same-block part-boundary facets: `resolveTextAnchor` maps seam display
  offsets to the left part by convention; a caret resting at a right
  part's offset 0 reports a different textId than its resolved anchor
  (same causal gap). Not yet observed in DST schedules.
- Passive exactness remains bounded to single-remote-frame steps.
- Word/line unit content stays platform-owned; phantom DOM-space spans
  are plausibility-bounded.

## Remediation IV — adversarial re-review round (composition/editable/settlement)

Six findings from a second adversarial pass over remediation III, all
reproduced and pinned:

- **Composition after split** — remediation III's stream-start fix
  right-bound the anchor (`a >= 0`), which moved the caret past
  same-gap inserts (`tail@0`→`tail@1`; reproduced as `nにello`). The
  encoding needed one more bit: assoc is a free number channel, and the
  engine treats every `assoc < 0` identically (left insert-affinity).
  `anchorAt` now mints `a: -2` at a mid-backing stream start — left
  insertion semantics preserved — and `resolveAnchor` reads `a <= -2`
  as "prefer the right-side owner facet" for display ownership. The
  dense test oracle mirrors the decode. Pins: two-replica remote insert
  at `tail@0` keeps the anchor at `tail@0` (anchors.test.ts), plus the
  DOM fixture `replaces intermediate composition text at a fresh split
block start` (`alpha|Hello` split → compose `に` via `n`) — fails
  with `nにello` under a plain right-bind, passes under `-2`.
- **Editable traversal** — `firstEditableText`/`lastEditableText` only
  descended `children.at(0)`/`at(-1)`: a leading noneditable divider
  lost the caret (`[alpha, list(beta), list(divider, gamma)]`). Both
  getters now scan all children (forward / reverse) preferring a
  rendered own text; the root fallback scans `root` itself rather than
  `children[0]`. Pins: forward (`list(divider, gamma)` → `gamma`,
  typing `Z` lands there) and backward (container ending in a divider →
  previous editable text's end), each verified to fail under the old
  single-child descent.
- **Stale-write bypasses** — two escaped the admission gate:
  `setAtTextOffset(text.id, …)` (string ids resolved `null` and skipped
  the guard) and `setAtRange`'s lookup-error branch (wrote the old
  range before the gesture check). `setAtTextOffset`'s predicate is now
  argument-free (gesture serial + state-vs-target/call snapshot for any
  `textOrId`); `setAtRange` and `setAtBlockRange` gate every write —
  catch fallback, foreign-focus fallback, DOM write, and the post-await
  reconcile — behind one `stale()`/`blockStale()` closure. Pins fail
  with each guard individually disabled.
- **Collab oracle joint range** — per-endpoint expectations wrongly
  rejected production's survivor collapse (`alpha@1 → beta@3`, `alpha`
  deleted → caret `beta@3` while the dead start "expected" `beta@0`).
  `acceptableSelectionShapes` now enumerates whole-selection shapes
  mirroring `writeResolved`: both anchors → resolved range, one anchor
  → caret at the survivor, none + a dead endpoint → start-block seam /
  root fallback, none + nothing dead → pre stands. The page dump now
  records `firstEditableText`/`lastEditableText` (not phantom-capable
  `firstText`) plus a `textOwners` map; `seamExpectation` continues past
  live-but-textless siblings like production's directional scan. Dump
  sanity now also rejects `isCollapsed` with unequal endpoints and any
  textId not owned by the claimed live block.
- **Command-result boundary** — the per-command expectation ran before
  deferred work settled, so a `setTimeout(100ms)` corruption passed the
  step and became the next one's baseline. Order is now: exec → flush →
  LOCAL quiesce → expectation → deliver → flush → REMOTE quiesce. The
  delayed-`CORRUPTION` canary sits next to the no-op canary and fails on
  the corrupted tree (verified: rejection carries the corruption diff).
- **Real quiescence** — `quiesce()` inspected the pending map before
  crossing a microtask boundary, so `queueMicrotask(() => setTimeout(cb,
10))` reported settlement with the callback still pending. It now
  awaits a microtask checkpoint before the first inspection and after
  every drain wait, and tracks `requestAnimationFrame`/`cancelAnimation-
Frame` as in-horizon callbacks. Probes pin both the microtask-queued
  timer and the rAF callback.

Audit — `selection.svelte.ts` recovery completeness: the git object
chain `e2b4252f → 0c44fa704 → e14fce762 → worktree` shows
`e14fce762` is the newest staged snapshot and diffs to the current file
by exactly this round's edits; `0c44fa704 → e14fce762` covers the
drifted-anchor restore, editable-text seam, `_drop` links, and the
gesture/`expectInternalFocus` guards. No pre-checkout edit is missing.

- `pnpm test:dst` **57/57** — full collab + solo seeds under the joint

- The `-2` assoc marker is application-level: engine code only ever

## Remediation V — browser drift-echo + word-delete consolidation round

Findings from the real-browser collab lanes (Firefox first, then
WebKit/Chromium), all reproduced in `tests/editor-dst/` seeds and pinned:

- **Render churn re-parks live carets (Gecko)** — any commit's render
  can mutate DOM under a live caret (delta re-split shortening a text
  node, keyed span remount via `domVersion`, `_setItems`/`_kill`
  re-render). Gecko clamps the caret to the surviving boundary; the
  trailing `selectionchange` then derives + re-mints anchors one atom
  off — a passive peer's caret walked a step on every remote commit.
  Two layered defenses:
  - `domSelectionChurnSeq` is bumped eagerly at the model→render
    boundary (`Text.attach`/destroy, `Text._setItems`, `_kill`, the
    per-span mutation observer) and baselined into
    `churnBaselineAtGesture` at each `markUserGesture` — churn stays
    outstanding for the whole inter-gesture window, covering Gecko's
    delayed re-park echo. `restoreDriftedEchoCaret` rejects an echo at
    an unchanged gesture serial with outstanding churn whose derived
    DOM position differs from the resolved anchors — reverting DOM ←
    model instead of deriving. A matching echo, an echo after a serial
    bump (real gesture), and an echo with no outstanding churn all
    derive normally, so foreign/programmatic writes are untouched.
  - `captureSelectionForRemoteApply` →
    `reconcileSelectionAfterRemoteApply` runs at the post-flush `tick()`
    after every commit: it resolves the captured anchors on the
    post-apply document and re-asserts DOM ← model before the echo's
    task lands. Both are skipped while `isHandlingUserInput` — inside a
    user-input window the DOM caret is the user's own write
    (autocorrect, IME, drop) and a commit may legitimately slide the
    captured anchors, so model→DOM would fight the user's caret.
    (Regression: the autocorrect fixture `teh| cat` → `the cat` had the
    reconcile dragging the caret 3→1.)
- **`scheduleCaretWriteVerification` self-fight** — two armed verifies
  targeting different offsets observed `backToPreWrite` after each
  other dragged state back, ping-ponging the caret; worse, a verify
  armed before a remote commit fired after it and misread the
  legitimate move as a reverted write. Fixed with two disarms: a
  `caretWriteEpoch` (a newer write supersedes) and a
  `_docCommitVersion` snapshot (an intervening commit owns the move,
  not the verify).
- **Word/line deletes adopted the browser's raw targetRange** —
  `syncSelectionFromBeforeInputTargetRange` adopted the full delivered
  range for `deleteWord*`/`deleteSoftLine*`/`deleteHardLine*`; Firefox
  delivers a block-level `endContainer` for word deletes, which derived
  to a degenerate caret and no-oped the delete. These inputTypes now
  take the `deleteContent*` contract: the delivered range locates the
  caret edge (start for forward, end for backward) and the model's
  `deleteCollapsed{Word,Line}*` computes the extent —
  `del.unit.caret-edge` in `docs/editor-delete-contract.md`.
- **Trailing boundary run unconsumable** — `getNext/PreviousWord*`
  returned the caret unchanged when only a boundary run remained in the
  direction (`"cc9· 9· "` caret@6, forward `"· "`), so the delete no-oped
  while the browser (and the oracle's legal-span bounds) counted it
  deletable. The boundary functions now return the boundary run's far
  edge — `del.unit.model-boundary` — matching the platform's own
  word-delete result. Oracle pins updated (`alpha bravo|` with
  delivered `[11,12]` deletes the contract unit `[6,12]`, yielding
  `"alpha "`).
- **Harness DOM-first placements are user decisions** —
  `setNativeSelection` (`src/tests/dom/test.utils.ts`) and the
  playwright collab runner's `addRange` placement now call
  `markUserGesture()` first; without the mark the drift gate reads
  their synthetic `selectionchange` as drift and reverts them.
- **Instrumented for the residual flake class** — `__selDrift` /
  `__EDYTOR_SEL_LOG__` probes (gesture marks, churn marks, commit
  captures, echo verdicts, caller hints) are armed only on the DST test
  page; all probe writes are flag-gated and no-ops in production —
  including the `new Error().stack` capture in `markUserGesture`, which
  no longer allocates on real pointer/key events. One low-rate
  Chromium/WebKit flake remains a watch item (a passive peer's
  collapsed caret once minted a range after reconnect); five
  consecutive Chromium collab-lane runs and a WebKit rerun did not
  reproduce it, and the probes will capture the admission path if it
  recurs.

### Validation (remediation V)

- `pnpm check` 0 errors/warnings; `pnpm lint` clean.
- `vitest --run` unit lane: **1950 passed** (123 files).
- `pnpm test:dom`: **346/346** — incl. `selection-write-dedupe`,
  `android-caret-restore`, `command-determinism`, and the autocorrect
  input-fallback fixture.
- `browser-state-oracle`: **48/48** after the collapse-edge pin updates.
- `pnpm test:dst` solo seeds: **8/8** across chromium/firefox/webkit.

  green — incl. five consecutive runs of the previously-flaky seed.
- CRDT random corpus: **470/470**.

### Remaining qualified bounds (remediation V)

- The drift guard keys on gesture-serial + outstanding-churn; a user
  input modality that moves the caret without any of
  pointerdown/keydown/beforeinput/focus would be indistinguishable from
  drift inside a churn window — none is known in the supported engines,
  but the watch item above is this class.
- Passive recovery exactness is verified against single-frame remote
  deliveries; multi-frame queued delivery inherits the same machinery
  but is exercised only via generated schedules.
